Consumers should treat the dataclass as the\n    canonical surface for the FR-G4 per-eval contract rather than\n    re-deriving filenames from string constants.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_dir: Path\n    logs_jsonl: Path\n    tty_transcript: Path\n    artifacts_dir: Path\n\n\ndef compose_per_eval_dir(run_dir: Path | str, eval_id: str) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Return ``<run_dir>/per-eval/<eval_id>/``.\n\n    Validates ``eval_id`` against the FR-SCH2 character set so a\n    crafted id cannot escape the per-eval subtree via path-traversal\n    components.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if not isinstance(eval_id, str) or not _EVAL_ID_RE.match(eval_id):\n        raise ValueError(\n            f\"eval_id {eval_id!r} fails the FR-SCH2 [A-Za-z0-9_.-]{{1,64}} guard\"\n        )\n    return Path(run_dir) / PER_EVAL_DIRNAME / eval_id\n\n\ndef allocate_per_eval_paths(\n    run_dir: Path | str,\n    eval_id: str,\n    *,\n    create: bool = True,\n) -> PerEvalPaths:\n    \"\"\"Return + optionally create the per-eval subtree paths.\n\n    When ``create=True`` (the default), the per-eval directory and the\n    ``artifacts/`` subdirectory are created via ``mkdir(parents=True,\n    exist_ok=True)``. The :data:`LOGS_JSONL_NAME` and\n    :data:`TTY_TRANSCRIPT_NAME` files are *not* touched — they are\n    opened lazily by the runner / PtyDriver on first write.\n\n    Idempotent: re-allocating an existing per-eval subtree returns the\n    same paths and leaves the on-disk state intact.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_dir = compose_per_eval_dir(run_dir, eval_id)\n    artifacts_dir = eval_dir / ARTIFACTS_SUBDIR_NAME\n    paths = PerEvalPaths(\n        eval_dir=eval_dir,\n        logs_jsonl=eval_dir / LOGS_JSONL_NAME,\n        tty_transcript=eval_dir / TTY_TRANSCRIPT_NAME,\n        artifacts_dir=artifacts_dir,\n    )\n    if create:\n        artifacts_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    return paths\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Inverse: parse a run-dir back into (date, run-id)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass RunDirComponents:\n    \"\"\"Parsed ``(date_segment, run_id)`` pair for a constructed run-dir.\"\"\"\n\n    date_segment: str\n    run_id: str\n\n\ndef parse_run_dir_components(run_dir: Path | str) -> RunDirComponents:\n    \"\"\"Return the ``(date_segment, run_id)`` pair encoded by ``run_dir``.\n\n    Walks the path looking for the ``.dev/eval-runs/`` prefix and takes\n    the next two segments as ``(date, run-id)``. Raises\n    :class:`ValueError` if the path does not match the FR-G4 layout —\n    useful as a structural assertion at the seam between the CLI and\n    the Reporter so a misrouted write fails loudly.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    parts = Path(run_dir).parts\n    # Walk back-to-front so a path like ``/.../.dev/eval-runs/<d>/<r>/...``\n    # still matches even if extra segments trail after the run-id.\n    eval_runs_idx: int | None = None\n    for i in range(len(parts) - 1):\n        if parts[i] == \".dev\" and i + 1 < len(parts) and parts[i + 1] == \"eval-runs\":\n            eval_runs_idx = i + 1\n    if eval_runs_idx is None:\n        raise ValueError(\n            f\"run_dir {run_dir!r} is not anchored at .dev/eval-runs/\"\n        )\n    if eval_runs_idx + 2 >= len(parts):\n        raise ValueError(\n            f\"run_dir {run_dir!r} is missing the <date>/<run-id> tail \"\n            \"under .dev/eval-runs/\"\n        )\n    return RunDirComponents(\n        date_segment=parts[eval_runs_idx + 1],\n        run_id=parts[eval_runs_idx + 2],\n    )\n","numLines":306,"startLine":1,"totalLines":306}}}

The dataclass is frozen so once a runner emits an\n289\t    outcome it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n290\t\n291\t    * ``eval_id``             — id of the expanded eval. The 8-field\n    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n\n    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. Same\n      typing rationale as ``expected``.\n    * ``message``     — human-readable one-liner summarising the diff;\n      defaults to ``\"\"`` so a caller can omit it when the diff between\n      ``expected`` / ``actual`` is self-explanatory.\n    * ``artifact_ref`` — optional path-or-URI to a side-car artifact\n      (rendered diff file, dumped JSONL, captured stderr). ``None`` when no\n      artifact was written. The Reporter resolves the ref against the run's\n      scratch root before emitting the JSON report.\n    * ``traceback``   — optional Python traceback string captured when the\n      Expect itself raised. ``None`` for assertion failures that produced a\n      clean diff rather than an exception.\n\n    The dataclass is frozen so a Reporter consumer cannot mutate failure\n    records mid-render. Equality is structural across all 8 fields, which\n    keeps deduplication straightforward.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    expect_id: str\n    expect_name: str\n    expected: Any\n    actual: Any\n    message: str = \"\"\n    artifact_ref: Optional[str] = None\n    traceback: Optional[str] = None\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-005.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS`` so\n        the output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or\n        dataclass internals. ``expected`` / ``actual`` are passed through\n        as-is — callers populate them with JSON-shaped payloads, so the\n        reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result directly.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return {name: getattr(self, name) for name in _EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS}\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable\n# across Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"title\",\n    \"status\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"expects\",\n    \"skip_reason\",\n    \"skip_flag_triggered\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n    \"error_class\",","numLines":80,"startLine":200,"totalLines":938}}}

Four FR-RPT1 +\n6\tDM-012 obligations have to hold for any caller that turns a\n7\t:class:`RunSummary` into bytes:\n8\t\n9\t1. **N'-vs-K equality** — ``len(summary.evals) ==\n10\t   counts.expanded_n_prime``. Four FR-RPT1 +\nDM-012 obligations have to hold for any caller that turns a\n:class:`RunSummary` into bytes:\n\n1. **N'-vs-K equality** — ``len(summary.evals) ==\n   counts.expanded_n_prime``. A summary that satisfies the dimensional\n   invariant must render through every emitter (``to_markdown``,\n   ``to_json``, ``to_yaml``, ``to_junit``) without raising.\n\n2. **SKIPPED inclusion** — design-spec §9 declares that SKIPPED rows\n   stay in ``evals[]`` (with ``skip_reason`` populated) so the row\n   accounting never tries to \"compress\" the array. The JSON the\n   Reporter writes must surface those rows verbatim — both at the row\n   level (``evals[].status == \"SKIPPED\"`` with ``skip_reason``) and at\n   the count level (``counts.skipped_s`` reflects the tally).\n\n3. **Mismatch failure → exit code 2** — when the invariant is\n   violated, every emitter raises :class:`ReporterContractViolation`\n   *before* writing any artefact, and the violation maps to process\n   exit code 2 (``REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE``). Design-spec\n   §4 classifies a Reporter contract violation as a harness contract\n   error: the orchestrator's accounting and the rendered ``evals[]``\n   disagree, so the run itself is suspect and no partial artefact is\n   allowed on disk.\n\n4. **JSON schema fidelity** — the JSON payload the Reporter renders\n   validates against ``summary.schema.json`` (DM-012 / T03.10). This\n   is the cross-link FR-RPT1 ↔ DM-012: the schema documents the shape\n   the Reporter contracts to write.\n\nThese four scenarios anchor TEST-007 in the phase-4 tasklist\n(``§T04.17``). The test module is deliberately self-contained — it\nbuilds RunSummary fixtures from scratch rather than reusing\n``test_run_report.py`` helpers — so a future refactor of the writer\nhelpers cannot Skipped until T04.10 wires the run-loop closure (the signal handler is installed inside that closure). |\n\n## 3. subprocess invocation\n\n`_run_eval(...)` invokes the **console-script** at\n`<venv>/bin/superclaude`, not `python -m superclaude`. The package\ndeliberately ships without a `__main__.py`; the console-script\ngenerated by hatchling is the canonical operator surface and matches\nhow CI / docs tell users to invoke the harness. The helper resolves\nthe binary off `sys.executable`'s parent so the test stays correct in\nany venv layout (CI, `make dev`, ephemeral worktrees) and skips\ngracefully if no console-script is installed on the host.\n\n## 4. Forward-dependency policy (T04.10 deferral)\n\nThe 0 / 1 / 3 paths all flow through the run-loop closure whose\nhelpers (`_new_run_id`, `_run_one_spec`, `_compute_run_stats`,\n`RUN_CLEAN_EXIT_CODE`, `RUN_FAILURES_EXIT_CODE`,\n`RUN_INTERRUPTED_EXIT_CODE`) are T04.10 deliverables. While those\nhelpers are absent the closure raises `NameError` before reaching the\nexit-code branch under test. Mirroring the\n`test_no_pty_exclusion.py` pattern (T04.16), each affected test calls\n`_skip_unless_t0410_landed()`, which returns the missing names and\nissues `pytest.skip(...)` with a self-clearing diagnostic.\n\nThe exit-code-2 test **always runs**. Flag validation at\n`commands.py:1398` (`--timeout-mult`) and `:1405` (`--max-disk-mb`)\nsits BEFORE the run-id helper, so the `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` path is\nexercised today regardless of T04.10 status.\n\nWhen T04.10 lands the skipped tests auto-clear and the suite then pins\nall four exit codes end-to-end at the process boundary.\n\n## 5. Exit code 2 mapping (Scenario 3 detail)\n\nDesign-spec §4 maps the four eval-run exit codes as follows:\n\n| Code | Constant(s) | Meaning |\n|---|---|---|\n| 0 | `RUN_CLEAN_EXIT_CODE` | All evals satisfied their `expects:` blocks; no FAIL / ERRORED / TIMEOUT / XPASS. |\n| 1 | `RUN_FAILURES_EXIT_CODE` | At least one eval failed; orchestrator finished cleanly. |\n| **2** | `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE`, `SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE`, `SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE`, `SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`, `EVAL_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE`, `COVERAGE_GATE_FAILED_EXIT_CODE`, `DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE`, `RUN_BODY_DEFERRED_EXIT_CODE`, `REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` | **Harness contract error — operator-supplied flag invalid, scratch-root violation, suite missing, capability gate breach, Reporter contract violation, or run-body deferred.** All these constants resolve to `2` and represent \"the harness rejected the run before / during dispatch.\" |\n| 3 | `RUN_INTERRUPTED_EXIT_CODE`, `signal_handler.EXIT_INTERRUPTED` | Operator interrupted the run (SIGINT/SIGTERM cooperative cancellation). |\n\nThe T04.17 / D-0078 Reporter contract suite pins\n`REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE == 2` + the exception type at\nthe **library boundary**. This test module pins\n`HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE == 2` and the process-level `returncode == 2`\ncontract at the **CLI boundary**. Together they prove the constant\nboth holds and flows.\n\n## 6. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01VqKVXone2EcEkXU3tYpfut","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_019rmTLPiVTyawX7ZtUYgPyb","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/tests/cli/eval/test_artifact_reproducibility.py","content":"\"\"\"TEST-009 artifact reproducibility tests (R-080 / D-0080 / T04.20).\n\nPins the FR-G4 + DM-012 reproducibility contract every ``superclaude\neval run`` invocation must satisfy at the artifact layer. The five\nacceptance criteria from ``phase-4-tasklist.md`` §T04.20 are:\n\n1. **Run dir matches the FR-G4 pattern** —\n   ``<output_root>/.dev/eval-runs/<YYYY-MM-DD>/<run-id>/`` and is\n   deterministic for a ``(started_at, suite_name)`` pair so two runs\n   on the same instant collide on the same path.\n2. **Per-eval ``logs.jsonl`` exists and is non-empty after a run** —\n   the per-eval JSONL emitter (T03.05 / D-0049) drops events under\n   the pinned filename and the file survives the run.\n3. **Per-eval ``tty.transcript`` exists** — the PtyDriver / PtyStream\n   capture lands at the pinned filename inside the per-eval subtree.\n4. **Stack trace recorded on ERRORED outcomes** — the per-eval JSONL\n   carries a ``traceback`` field on the failure event AND the\n   Reporter-rendered ``summary.json`` carries the same traceback\n   inside the ``evals[].expects[].failure.traceback`` field (DM-005).\n5. **summary.json ``evals[]`` cross-links to per-eval artifact paths** —\n   each row's ``artifacts`` mapping names the per-eval ``logs.jsonl``,\n   ``tty.transcript``, and ``artifacts/`` directory using paths\n   relative to the run directory so a reviewer can navigate from\n   summary → per-eval transcript without re-deriving the layout.\n\nThe module is deliberately self-contained: it builds RunSummary\nfixtures from scratch and writes per-eval artifacts via the\n:mod:`superclaude.cli.eval.artifact_layout` helpers (the FR-G4\nsource-of-truth). The full ``superclaude eval run`` end-to-end loop\nis a T04.10 forward dependency; by anchoring the contract at the\nlayout + Reporter seam this module pins the reproducibility matrix\ntoday and the T04.10 closure must satisfy the same invariants when\nit lands.\n\nCross-links:\n* FR-G4 / R-074 / D-0074 / T04.13 — the artifact-layout module\n  (``compose_run_dir``, ``allocate_per_eval_paths``,\n  ``parse_run_dir_components``) this test exercises.\n* DM-012 / T03.10 — the summary.schema.json the Reporter renders to;\n  ``evals[].artifacts`` is the cross-link field this test reads.\n* DM-005 / T01.16 — ``ExpectFailure.traceback`` is the rendered\n  traceback channel; ``logs.jsonl`` carries the same string verbatim\n  so the JSONL log is the authoritative source the schema docs name.\n* T04.17 / D-0078 (Reporter contract suite) — sibling tests that pin\n  the N'-vs-K invariant; this module trusts that guard and focuses\n  on the artifact + cross-link contract.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport re\nimport traceback as tb_mod\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.artifact_layout import (\n    ARTIFACTS_SUBDIR_NAME,\n    LOGS_JSONL_NAME,\n    PER_EVAL_DIRNAME,\n    RUN_DIR_PREFIX,\n    TTY_TRANSCRIPT_NAME,\n    allocate_per_eval_paths,\n    compose_per_eval_dir,\n    compose_run_dir,\n    compose_run_id,\n    parse_run_dir_components,\n)\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.models import (\n    EvalOutcome,\n    ExpectFailure,\n    ExpectResult,\n    RunCounts,\n    RunSummary,\n    RunTotals,\n)\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.reporter import Reporter\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Layout pattern constants (kept identical to artifact_layout's pins so a\n# rename there breaks here intentionally).\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n_RUN_ID_RE = re.compile(r\"^\\d{6}Z-[0-9a-f]{8}$\")\n_DATE_SEGMENT_RE = re.compile(r\"^\\d{4}-\\d{2}-\\d{2}$\")\n\n# Fixture inputs used across the matrix. Kept as module-level constants so\n# every test exercises the *same* (started_at, suite_name) pair and the\n# determinism assertions in test_run_dir_deterministic_for_inputs and\n# test_summary_json_cross_links_per_eval_artifacts can compare against the\n# same expected run-id.\n_STARTED_AT = \"2026-05-20T15:58:38Z\"\n_FINISHED_AT = \"2026-05-20T15:59:30Z\"\n_SUITE_NAME = \"real\"\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Fixture helpers — build a complete FR-G4 artifact tree on disk\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _make_traceback() -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return a realistic Python traceback string.\n\n    Raises and catches a ``RuntimeError`` so the resulting traceback\n    contains live frame lines (``File \"...\", line ...``) rather than a\n    hand-crafted string a future change could let pass while losing the\n    actual stack content.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    try:\n        raise RuntimeError(\"simulated harness error inside eval body\")\n    except RuntimeError as exc:\n        return \"\".join(\n            tb_mod.format_exception(type(exc), exc, exc.__traceback__)\n        )\n\n\ndef _write_pass_per_eval(\n    run_dir: Path,\n    eval_id: str = \"E1\",\n) -> dict[str, Path]:\n    \"\"\"Materialise the per-eval subtree for a PASS scenario.\n\n    Writes a minimal JSONL stream (start / expect / end events) and a\n    short TTY transcript so the layer-level assertions can confirm both\n    files exist and carry content. Returns a mapping of the three\n    cross-link names the Reporter will surface in ``evals[].artifacts``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    paths = allocate_per_eval_paths(run_dir, eval_id)\n\n    paths.logs_jsonl.write_text(\n        \"\\n\".join(\n            [\n                json.dumps(\n                    {\n                        \"event\": \"eval.start\",\n                        \"eval_id\": eval_id,\n                        \"ts\": _STARTED_AT,\n                    }\n                ),\n                json.dumps(\n                    {\n                        \"event\": \"expect.pass\",\n                        \"eval_id\": eval_id,\n                        \"name\": \"exit_code\",\n                    }\n                ),\n                json.dumps(\n                    {\n                        \"event\": \"eval.end\",\n                        \"eval_id\": eval_id,\n                        \"ts\": _FINISHED_AT,\n                        \"status\": \"PASS\",\n                    }\n                ),\n            ]\n        )\n        + \"\\n\",\n        encoding=\"utf-8\",\n    )\n\n    paths.tty_transcript.write_text(\n        f\"$ claude --eval {eval_id}\\nready\\n[exit 0]\\n\",\n        encoding=\"utf-8\",\n    )\n\n    return {\n        \"logs.jsonl\": paths.logs_jsonl,\n        \"tty.transcript\": paths.tty_transcript,\n        \"artifacts/\": paths.artifacts_dir,\n    }\n\n\ndef _write_errored_per_eval(\n    run_dir: Path,\n    eval_id: str = \"E2\",\n) -> tuple[dict[str, Path], str]:\n    \"\"\"Materialise the per-eval subtree for an ERRORED scenario.\n\n    Returns a ``(paths, traceback_string)`` pair so callers can both\n    cross-link the artifacts and confirm the traceback round-trips\n    verbatim into the JSONL log + Reporter output.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    paths = allocate_per_eval_paths(run_dir, eval_id)\n    trace = _make_traceback()\n\n    paths.logs_jsonl.write_text(\n        \"\\n\".join(\n            [\n                json.dumps(\n                    {\n                        \"event\": \"eval.start\",\n                        \"eval_id\": eval_id,\n                        \"ts\": _STARTED_AT,\n                    }\n                ),\n                json.dumps(\n                    {\n                        \"event\": \"eval.error\",\n                        \"eval_id\": eval_id,\n                        \"error_class\": \"builtins.RuntimeError\",\n                        \"message\": \"simulated harness error inside eval body\",\n                        \"traceback\": trace,\n                    }\n                ),\n                json.dumps(\n                    {\n                        \"event\": \"eval.end\",\n                        \"eval_id\": eval_id,\n                        \"ts\": _FINISHED_AT,\n                        \"status\": \"ERRORED\",\n                    }\n                ),\n            ]\n        )\n        + \"\\n\",\n        encoding=\"utf-8\",\n    )\n\n    paths.tty_transcript.write_text(\n        f\"$ claude --eval {eval_id}\\nready\\nTraceback (most recent call last):\\n\",\n        encoding=\"utf-8\",\n    )\n\n    return (\n        {\n            \"logs.jsonl\": paths.logs_jsonl,\n            \"tty.transcript\": paths.tty_transcript,\n            \"artifacts/\": paths.artifacts_dir,\n        },\n        trace,\n    )\n\n\ndef _per_eval_artifacts_map(\n    run_dir: Path,\n    eval_id: str,\n) -> dict[str, str]:\n    \"\"\"Return the run-relative artifact paths the Reporter cross-links.\n\n    Mirrors the convention DM-001 / DM-012 declare: ``artifacts`` values\n    are run-relative POSIX strings so the JSON is portable across hosts.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_dir = compose_per_eval_dir(run_dir, eval_id)\n    return {\n        LOGS_JSONL_NAME: (\n            eval_dir.relative_to(run_dir) / LOGS_JSONL_NAME\n        ).as_posix(),\n        TTY_TRANSCRIPT_NAME: (\n            eval_dir.relative_to(run_dir) / TTY_TRANSCRIPT_NAME\n        ).as_posix(),\n        ARTIFACTS_SUBDIR_NAME: (\n            eval_dir.relative_to(run_dir) / ARTIFACTS_SUBDIR_NAME\n        ).as_posix(),\n    }\n\n\ndef _build_summary(\n    *,\n    run_id: str,\n    pass_eval_id: str,\n    pass_artifacts: dict[str, str],\n    errored_eval_id: str,\n    errored_artifacts: dict[str, str],\n    traceback_str: str,\n) -> RunSummary:\n    \"\"\"Construct a two-row RunSummary (PASS + ERRORED) for the matrix.\n\n    The pass row's ``artifacts`` maps the three FR-G4 entries; the\n    errored row carries an :class:`ExpectFailure` with the same\n    traceback string the JSONL log holds so callers can confirm the\n    cross-channel cross-link.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    pass_outcome = EvalOutcome(\n        eval_id=pass_eval_id,\n        title=f\"{pass_eval_id} pass\",\n        status=\"PASS\",\n        duration_sec=1.25,\n        expects=(\n            ExpectResult(name=\"exit_code\", passed=True, message=\"ok\"),\n        ),\n        artifacts=pass_artifacts,\n    )\n\n    errored_failure = ExpectFailure(\n        eval_id=errored_eval_id,\n        expect_id=\"harness[0]\",\n        expect_name=\"harness\",\n        expected=None,\n        actual=None,\n        message=\"harness raised RuntimeError\",\n        artifact_ref=errored_artifacts[LOGS_JSONL_NAME],\n        traceback=traceback_str,\n    )\n    errored_outcome = EvalOutcome(\n        eval_id=errored_eval_id,\n        title=f\"{errored_eval_id} errored\",\n        status=\"ERRORED\",\n        duration_sec=0.5,\n        expects=(\n            ExpectResult(\n                name=\"harness\",\n                passed=False,\n                message=\"harness raised RuntimeError\",\n                failure=errored_failure,\n            ),\n        ),\n        artifacts=errored_artifacts,\n        error_class=\"builtins.RuntimeError\",\n    )\n\n    counts = RunCounts(\n        manifest_n=2,\n        expanded_n_prime=2,\n        kept_k=2,\n        skipped_s=0,\n        kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime=True,\n    )\n    totals = RunTotals(passed=1, errored=1)\n\n    return RunSummary(\n        run_id=run_id,\n        started_at=_STARTED_AT,\n        finished_at=_FINISHED_AT,\n        duration_sec=52.0,\n        suite=f\"suites/{_SUITE_NAME}.yaml\",\n        manifest_version=\"1.0\",\n        parallel=1,\n        counts=counts,\n        totals=totals,\n        evals=(pass_outcome, errored_outcome),\n    )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Scenario 1 — Run-dir pattern matches `.dev/eval-runs/<ISO>/<run-id>/`\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_run_dir_matches_fr_g4_pattern(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"FR-G4 AC: run-dir is anchored under ``.dev/eval-runs/<date>/<run-id>/``.\n\n    Pins the three structural invariants the rest of the matrix\n    depends on:\n\n    * The path is rooted at :data:`RUN_DIR_PREFIX` (``.dev/eval-runs``).\n    * The date segment is ``YYYY-MM-DD`` (derived from ``started_at``).\n    * The run-id matches the ``<HHMMSSZ>-<8-hex>`` shape.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    run_dir = compose_run_dir(tmp_path, _STARTED_AT, suite_name=_SUITE_NAME)\n\n    # Path is rooted at .dev/eval-runs/.\n    assert RUN_DIR_PREFIX.as_posix() in run_dir.as_posix()\n\n    # Decomposed: <output_root>/.dev/eval-runs/<YYYY-MM-DD>/<run-id>/\n    rel = run_dir.relative_to(tmp_path).parts\n    assert rel[0] == \".dev\"\n    assert rel[1] == \"eval-runs\"\n    assert _DATE_SEGMENT_RE.fullmatch(rel[2]), rel[2]\n    assert _RUN_ID_RE.fullmatch(rel[3]), rel[3]\n\n    # parse_run_dir_components round-trips the pair.\n    parsed = parse_run_dir_components(run_dir)\n    assert parsed.date_segment == \"2026-05-20\"\n    assert parsed.run_id == compose_run_id(_STARTED_AT, _SUITE_NAME)\n\n\ndef test_run_dir_deterministic_for_inputs(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Reproducibility: same ``(started_at, suite_name)`` -> same run-dir.\n\n    The FR-G4 spec promises run-id determinism so two replayed runs\n    against the same suite at the same instant land on the same path\n    (a precondition for cache-style behaviour and idempotent CI\n    re-runs). Different inputs MUST differ — both axes are checked\n    here.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    a = compose_run_dir(tmp_path, _STARTED_AT, suite_name=_SUITE_NAME)\n    b = compose_run_dir(tmp_path, _STARTED_AT, suite_name=_SUITE_NAME)\n    assert a == b\n\n    # Different suite name -> different run-dir (hash tail diverges).\n    c = compose_run_dir(tmp_path, _STARTED_AT, suite_name=\"other\")\n    assert a != c\n    assert a.parent == c.parent  # Same date segment.\n\n    # Different timestamp -> different run-dir.\n    d = compose_run_dir(tmp_path, \"2026-05-20T15:58:39Z\", suite_name=_SUITE_NAME)\n    assert a != d\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Scenario 2 — Per-eval logs.jsonl exists, non-empty, JSON-Lines parsable\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_per_eval_logs_jsonl_present_and_parsable(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"FR-G4 AC: per-eval ``logs.jsonl`` exists and is JSONL-parsable.\n\n    The JSONL emitter (T03.05) writes one event per line. The\n    reproducibility contract requires (a) the file exists at the\n    pinned filename, (b) the file is non-empty, and (c) every line\n    parses as JSON so downstream parsers (CI dashboards, the\n    `Expect.jsonl` primitive) do not blow up on a corrupt run.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    run_dir = compose_run_dir(tmp_path, _STARTED_AT, suite_name=_SUITE_NAME)\n    paths = _write_pass_per_eval(run_dir, eval_id=\"E1\")\n\n    logs = paths[\"logs.jsonl\"]\n    assert logs.is_file(), \"logs.jsonl must exist at the FR-G4 pinned path\"\n    assert logs.name == LOGS_JSONL_NAME\n    raw = logs.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    assert raw.strip(), \"logs.jsonl must be non-empty after a run\"\n\n    events = [json.loads(line) for line in raw.splitlines() if line.strip()]\n    assert len(events) == 3, \"PASS fixture writes start/expect/end events\"\n    assert events[0][\"event\"] == \"eval.start\"\n    assert events[-1][\"status\"] == \"PASS\"\n\n    # And the file sits under the per-eval subtree (defense in depth: a\n    # refactor that placed logs.jsonl outside per-eval/ would skip this\n    # assertion silently if we only checked the name).\n    eval_dir = compose_per_eval_dir(run_dir, \"E1\")\n    assert logs.parent == eval_dir\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Scenario 3 — Per-eval tty.transcript exists and carries content\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_per_eval_tty_transcript_present_and_non_empty(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"FR-G4 AC: per-eval ``tty.transcript`` exists at the pinned filename.\n\n    The PtyDriver / PtyStream layer (T02.16 / T02.17) writes the raw\n    PTY capture to ``tty.transcript``. The reproducibility contract\n    requires the file exists, is non-empty, and lives inside the\n    per-eval subtree.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    run_dir = compose_run_dir(tmp_path, _STARTED_AT, suite_name=_SUITE_NAME)\n    paths = _write_pass_per_eval(run_dir, eval_id=\"E1\")\n\n    transcript = paths[\"tty.transcript\"]\n    assert transcript.is_file(), \"tty.transcript must exist at the FR-G4 path\"\n    assert transcript.name == TTY_TRANSCRIPT_NAME\n    body = transcript.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    assert body, \"tty.transcript must be non-empty after a run\"\n\n    eval_dir = compose_per_eval_dir(run_dir, \"E1\")\n    assert transcript.parent == eval_dir\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Scenario 4 — Stack trace recorded on ERRORED outcomes\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_errored_outcome_records_stack_trace(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"FR-G4 AC: ERRORED outcomes record the harness traceback.\n\n    DM-005 names ``ExpectFailure.traceback`` as the rendered\n    traceback channel and the design-spec notes the per-eval JSONL is\n    the authoritative source. Both surfaces must carry the *same*\n    traceback string so a reviewer can chase the failure from\n    summary.json into logs.jsonl without parsing format differences.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    run_dir = wrote.\n    events = [\n        json.loads(line)\n        for line in paths[\"logs.jsonl\"].read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\").splitlines()\n        if line.strip()\n    ]\n    error_events = [e for e in expected_trace\n    # Must contain a real Python frame line — guards against a future\n    # change that records an empty string under the right key.\n    assert \"Traceback (most recent call last):\" traceback_str=expected_trace,\n    )\n    payload = json.loads(Reporter(summary).to_json())\n\n    errored_row = next(\n        row for row in payload[\"evals\"] if row[\"status\"] == \"ERRORED\"\n    )\n    assert errored_row[\"error_class\"] == \"builtins.RuntimeError\"\n    assert errored_row[\"expects\"], \"ERRORED row must carry an ExpectResult\"\n    rendered_failure = errored_row[\"expects\"][0][\"failure\"]\n    assert rendered_failure is not None\n    assert rendered_failure[\"traceback\"] == expected_trace, (\n        \"summary.json and logs.jsonl must carry the ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_summary_json_cross_links_per_eval_artifacts(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"FR-G4 AC: ``summary.json  \"\"\"FR-G4 AC: run-dir is anchored under ``.dev/eval-runs/<date>/<run-id>/``.\n\n    Pins the three structural invariants the rest of the matrix\n    depends on:\n\n    * The path is rooted at assert parsed.date_segment == \"2026-05-20\"\n    assert parsed.run_id == compose_run_id(_STARTED_AT, _SUITE_NAME)\n\n\ndef test_run_dir_deterministic_for_inputs(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Reproducibility: same ``(started_at, suite_name)`` -> same run-dir.\n\n    The FR-G4 spec promises run-id determinism so two replayed runs\n    against the same suite at the same instant land on the same path\n    (a precondition for cache-style behaviour and idempotent CI\n    re-runs). Different inputs MUST differ — both axes are checked\n    here.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    a = compose_run_dir(tmp_path, _STARTED_AT, suite_name=_SUITE_NAME)\n    b = compose_run_dir(tmp_path, _STARTED_AT, suite_name=_SUITE_NAME)\n    assert a == b\n\n    # Different suite name -> different run-dir (hash tail diverges).\n    c = compose_run_dir(tmp_path, _STARTED_AT, suite_name=\"other\")\n    assert a != c\n    assert a.parent == c.parent  # Same date segment.\n\n    # Different timestamp -> different run-dir.\n    d = compose_run_dir(tmp_path, \"2026-05-20T15:58:39Z\", suite_name=_SUITE_NAME)\n    assert a != d\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Scenario 2 — Per-eval logs.jsonl exists, non-empty, JSON-Lines parsable\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_per_eval_logs_jsonl_present_and_parsable(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"FR-G4 AC: per-eval ``logs.jsonl`` exists and is JSONL-parsable.\n\n    The JSONL emitter (T03.05) writes one event per line. The\n    reproducibility contract requires (a) the file exists at the\n    pinned filename, (b) the file is non-empty, and (c) every line\n    parses as JSON so downstream parsers (CI dashboards, the\n    `Expect.jsonl` primitive) do not blow up on a corrupt run.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    run_dir = compose_run_dir(tmp_path, _STARTED_AT, suite_name=_SUITE_NAME)\n    paths = _write_pass_per_eval(run_dir, eval_id=\"E1\")\n\n    logs = paths[\"logs.jsonl\"]\n    assert logs.is_file(), \"logs.jsonl must exist at the FR-G4 pinned path\"\n    assert logs.name == LOGS_JSONL_NAME\n    raw = logs.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    assert raw.strip(), \"logs.jsonl must be non-empty after a run\"\n\n    events = [json.loads(line) for line in raw.splitlines() if line.strip()]\n    assert len(events) == 3, \"PASS fixture writes start/expect/end events\"\n    assert events[0][\"event\"] == \"eval.start\"\n    assert events[-1][\"status\"] == \"PASS\"\n\n    # And the file sits under the per-eval subtree (defense in depth: a\n    # refactor that placed logs.jsonl outside per-eval/ would skip this\n    # assertion silently if we only checked the name).\n    eval_dir = compose_per_eval_dir(run_dir, \"E1\")\n    assert logs.parent == eval_dir\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Scenario 3 — Per-eval tty.transcript exists and carries content\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_per_eval_tty_transcript_present_and_non_empty(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"FR-G4 AC: per-eval ``tty.transcript`` exists at the pinned filename.\n\n    The PtyDriver / PtyStream layer (T02.16 / T02.17) writes the raw\n    PTY capture to ``tty.transcript``. Goal\n15\t\n16\tPin the four exit codes named in design-spec §4 at the **process\n17\tboundary** that operators, CI jobs, and downstream pipelines actually\n18\tread (subprocess `returncode`). The library boundary already has its\n19\town pins — `REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` is held by the\n20\tT04.17 / D-0078 contract suite; `EXIT_INTERRUPTED` is held by the\n21\tT03.07 / D-0050 signal-handler suite — but neither of those verifies\n22\tthat the constant actually flows out through `sys.exit` and into the\n23\tshell. Every E1-E15 spec is tagged `no_pty: skip` (DOC-OQ3 / D-0077), so `--no-pty` short-circuits the whole suite into SKIPPED outcomes — no failures, no breaches, exit `0`. Skipped until T04.10 wires the run-loop closure (see §4). |\n37\t| 2 | **Failing run** | `test_exit_code_1_failing_run` | A synthetic single-eval suite whose `expect:` block is wired to fail is run via `superclaude eval run`; the orchestrator completes cleanly with one FAIL outcome, so the dispatcher exits `1` per design-spec §4. Skipped until T04.10 wires `_run_one_spec`. |\n38\t| 3 | **Harness contract error** | `test_exit_code_2_harness_error` | Two independent flag-validation gates (`--timeout-mult 0` and `--max-disk-mb -1`) each surface `eval run: --<flag> ...` on stderr and exit `2`. Both gates run BEFORE the run-loop closure, so this test runs today and is the canonical pin for the operator-visible exit-`2` contract. The test also pins `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE == 2` at the library boundary so a constant rename cannot drift the contract silently. |\n39\t| 4 | **Interrupted run** | `test_exit_code_3_interrupted_run` | `subprocess.Popen` spawns the run, gives the orchestrator 2 s to install signal handlers, then delivers `SIGINT`. The cooperative cancellation path (NFR-REL1 / T03.07) drains and the child exits with code `3`. The test also pins `EXIT_INTERRUPTED == 3` at the library boundary. Skipped until T04.10 wires the run-loop closure (the signal handler is installed inside that closure). |\n40\t\n41\t## 3. subprocess invocation\n42\t\n43\t`_run_eval(...)` invokes the **console-script** at\n44\t`<venv>/bin/superclaude`, not `python -m superclaude`. The package\n45\tdeliberately ships without a `__main__.py`; the console-script\n46\tgenerated by hatchling is the canonical operator surface and matches\n47\thow CI / docs tell users to invoke the harness. The helper resolves\n48\tthe binary off `sys.executable`'s parent so the test stays correct in\n49\tany venv layout (CI, `make dev`, ephemeral worktrees) and skips\n50\tgracefully if no console-script is installed on the host.\n51\t\n52\t## 4. Forward-dependency policy (T04.10 deferral)\n53\t\n54\tThe 0 / 1 / 3 paths all flow through the run-loop closure whose\n55\thelpers (`_new_run_id`, `_run_one_spec`, `_compute_run_stats`,\n56\t`RUN_CLEAN_EXIT_CODE`, `RUN_FAILURES_EXIT_CODE`,\n57\t`RUN_INTERRUPTED_EXIT_CODE`) are T04.10 deliverables. While those\n58\thelpers are absent the closure raises `NameError` before reaching the\n59\texit-code branch under test. Mirroring the\n60\t`test_no_pty_exclusion.py` pattern (T04.16), each affected test calls\n61\t`_skip_unless_t0410_landed()`, which returns the missing names and\n62\tissues `pytest.skip(...)` with a self-clearing diagnostic.\n63\t\n64\tThe exit-code-2 test **always runs**. Goal\n\nPin the four exit codes named in design-spec §4 at the **process\nboundary** that operators, CI jobs, and downstream pipelines actually\nread (subprocess `returncode`). The library boundary already has its\nown pins — `REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` is held by the\nT04.17 / D-0078 contract suite; `EXIT_INTERRUPTED` is held by the\nT03.07 / D-0050 signal-handler suite — but neither of those verifies\nthat the constant actually flows out through `sys.exit` and into the\nshell. This test module closes that gap, so a future refactor that\ndrops the constant on the way to `sys.exit` (or maps it to the wrong\nexit branch) fails loudly here rather than silently in CI summary\nparsers.\n\n## 2. Test matrix\n\nThe four pytest cases in `tests/cli/eval/test_exit_codes.py`\ncorrespond one-to-one with the TEST-008 scenarios named in the phase-4\ntasklist:\n\n| # | Scenario | Test function | Pins |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | **Clean run** | `test_exit_code_0_clean_run` | `superclaude eval run --suite real --no-pty --output-dir <tmp>` exits `0`. Every E1-E15 spec is tagged `no_pty: skip` (DOC-OQ3 / D-0077), so `--no-pty` short-circuits the whole suite into SKIPPED outcomes — no failures, no breaches, exit `0`. Skipped until T04.10 wires the run-loop closure (see §4). |\n| 2 | **Failing run** | `test_exit_code_1_failing_run` | A synthetic single-eval suite whose `expect:` block is wired to fail is run via `superclaude eval run`; the orchestrator completes cleanly with one FAIL outcome, so the dispatcher exits `1` per design-spec §4. Skipped until T04.10 wires `_run_one_spec`. |\n| 3 | **Harness contract error** | `test_exit_code_2_harness_error` | Two independent flag-validation gates (`--timeout-mult 0` and `--max-disk-mb -1`) each surface `eval run: --<flag> ...` on stderr and exit `2`. Both gates run BEFORE the run-loop closure, so this test runs today and is the canonical pin for the operator-visible exit-`2` contract. The test also pins `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE == 2` at the library boundary so a constant rename cannot drift the contract silently. |\n| 4 | **Interrupted run** | `test_exit_code_3_interrupted_run` | `subprocess.Popen` spawns the run, gives the orchestrator 2 s to install signal handlers, then delivers `SIGINT`. The cooperative cancellation path (NFR-REL1 / T03.07) drains and the child exits with code `3`. The test also pins `EXIT_INTERRUPTED == 3` at the library boundary. Skipped until T04.10 wires the run-loop closure (the signal handler is installed inside that closure). |\n\n## 3. subprocess invocation\n\n`_run_eval(...)` invokes the **console-script** at\n`<venv>/bin/superclaude`, not `python -m superclaude`. The package\ndeliberately ships without a `__main__.py`; the console-script\ngenerated by hatchling is the canonical operator surface and matches\nhow CI / docs tell users to invoke the harness. The helper resolves\nthe binary off `sys.executable`'s parent so the test stays correct in\nany venv layout (CI, `make dev`, ephemeral worktrees) and skips\ngracefully if no console-script is installed on the host.\n\n## 4. Forward-dependency policy (T04.10 deferral)\n\nThe 0 / 1 / 3 paths all flow through the run-loop closure whose\nhelpers (`_new_run_id`, `_run_one_spec`, `_compute_run_stats`,\n`RUN_CLEAN_EXIT_CODE`, `RUN_FAILURES_EXIT_CODE`,\n`RUN_INTERRUPTED_EXIT_CODE`) are T04.10 deliverables. While those\nhelpers are absent the closure raises `NameError` before reaching the\nexit-code branch under test. Mirroring the\n`test_no_pty_exclusion.py` pattern (T04.16), each affected test calls\n`_skip_unless_t0410_landed()`, which returns the missing names and\nissues `pytest.skip(...)` with a self-clearing diagnostic.\n\nThe exit-code-2 test **always runs**. Flag validation at\n`commands.py:1398` (`--timeout-mult`) and `:1405` (`--max-disk-mb`)\nsits BEFORE the run-id helper, so the `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` path is\nexercised today regardless of T04.10 status.\n\nWhen T04.10 lands the skipped tests auto-clear and the suite then pins\nall four exit codes end-to-end at the process boundary.\n\n## 5. Goal\n\nPin the **reproducible artifact layout** every `superclaude eval run`\ninvocation must produce so that:\n\n1. Two operators (or CI shards) replaying the same suite at the same\n   wall-clock instant land on **byte-identical** trees — no drift in\n   path shape, no drift in the per-eval emitter filenames, no drift in\n   the summary.json cross-link strings.\n2. An ERRORED eval **always** carries a Python traceback through both\n   the JSONL event log (live, append-only) and the rendered\n   `summary.json` (post-run, archived). A future refactor that loses\n   the traceback at either layer fails loudly here.\n3. The `summary.json` `evals[]` entries name per-eval artifact paths\n   relative to the run directory — so a reviewer can `cat` a\n   transcript from the summary without re-deriving the FR-G4 layout\n   formula.\n\nThe full end-to-end `superclaude eval run` orchestrator loop is a\nT04.10 forward dependency. This module deliberately anchors the\ncontract at the **layout + Reporter seam**: it builds the FR-G4 tree\nvia the `artifact_layout` helpers directly, materializes per-eval\nfiles, then drives `Reporter.write()`. The T04.10 closure must satisfy\nthe same invariants when it lands; nothing in this test couples to a\nspecific run-loop wiring.\n\n## 2. Reproducibility matrix\n\nThe eight pytest cases in `tests/cli/eval/test_artifact_reproducibility.py`\ncover the five acceptance criteria from phase-4-tasklist.md §T04.20\nplus three reinforcing guards. Cross-reference:\n\n| # | Test function | AC covered | Pins |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | `test_run_dir_matches_fr_g4_pattern` | AC-1: run dir matches `.dev/eval-runs/<ISO>/<run-id>/` | Regex pin on `<output_root>/.dev/eval-runs/YYYY-MM-DD/HHMMSSZ-<8-hex>/`. `parse_run_dir_components` round-trips `(date, run_id)` from the path. |\n| 2 | `test_run_dir_deterministic_for_inputs` | AC-1 + reproducibility invariant | Same `(suite_name, started_at)` → byte-identical run dir. Different `suite_name` OR different `started_at` → different run-id (sha256-deterministic per T04.13). |\n| 3 | `test_per_eval_logs_jsonl_present_and_parsable` | AC-2: `logs.jsonl` exists per eval | The JSONL lives at the pinned filename inside `per-eval/<eval_id>/`, every line is JSON-parseable, the file is non-empty. |\n| 4 | `test_per_eval_tty_transcript_present_and_non_empty` | AC-3: `tty.transcript` exists per eval | `tty.transcript` lands at the pinned filename inside `per-eval/<eval_id>/`, non-empty bytes. |\n| 5 | `test_errored_outcome_records_stack_trace` | AC-4: traceback on ERRORED | A real `RuntimeError` is raised + captured; the resulting traceback appears VERBATIM in both (a) the JSONL `result.failure` event and (b) the rendered `summary.json` `evals[].expects[0].failure.traceback`. |\n| 6 | `test_summary_json_cross_links_per_eval_artifacts` | AC-5: `evals[].artifacts` cross-link | Every `evals[]` row's `artifacts` mapping names `logs.jsonl`, `tty.transcript`, and the `artifacts/` directory; each cited POSIX path resolves to a real file/dir under the run directory. |\n| 7 | `test_artifact_tree_reproducible_across_replays` | AC-1 + AC-5 reinforcing | Two independent replays of the same `(suite_name, started_at, eval set)` produce **byte-identical** `summary.json` outputs (modulo the run-dir prefix), proving the cross-link strings are stable across replays. |\n| 8 | `test_parse_run_dir_rejects_non_layout_paths` | Negative guard | `parse_run_dir_components` raises on paths that do not match the FR-G4 layout — guards against silent acceptance of malformed paths. |\n\n## 3. FR-G4 layout pinned by this module\n\n```\n<output_root>/\n└── .dev/eval-runs/                           # RUN_DIR_PREFIX\n    └── <YYYY-MM-DD>/                         # date component of started_at\n        └── <HHMMSSZ>-<8-hex>/                # run_id = compose_run_id(suite_name, started_at)\n            ├── summary.json                  # Reporter.write target (DM-012)\n            └── per-eval/                     # PER_EVAL_DIRNAME\n                └── <eval_id>/                # one subtree per EvalOutcome\n                    ├── logs.jsonl            # LOGS_JSONL_NAME\n                    ├── tty.transcript        # TTY_TRANSCRIPT_NAME\n                    └── artifacts/            # ARTIFACTS_SUBDIR_NAME\n```\n\nThe `<8-hex>` suffix of the run-id is `sha256(suite_name || ISO-Z-instant)[:8]` —\ndeterministic, so two replays collide on the same path. This is the\nload-bearing reproducibility guarantee tests 1, 2, and 7 exercise.\n\n## 4. Cross-link contract (AC-5 detail)\n\nPer DM-001, each `EvalOutcome.artifacts` is a `Mapping[str, str]`\nwhere:\n- keys are the well-known names `\"logs.jsonl\"`, `\"tty.transcript\"`,\n  `\"artifacts\"`;\n- values are POSIX paths **relative to the run directory** (not\n  absolute, not relative to CWD).\n\nTest 6 verifies the cross-link by resolving every value against the\nrun directory and asserting the resulting path exists. The\n\"relative-to-run-dir\" convention is what makes the artifact tree\n**relocatable**: a CI job can tar the run dir and a reviewer can\nextract it anywhere on disk, and every cross-link in `summary.json`\nstill resolves.\n\n## 5. The `_per_eval_artifacts_map` helper enforces\n  this when building fixtures: it composes each per-eval file path,\n  takes the `.relative_to(run_dir)`, and converts to POSIX with\n  `.as_posix()`. Without this, a future\n  loosening of the parser could silently accept malformed paths and\n  the round-trip in test 1 would still pass. A hand-crafted string would have proven only that the test\n  agrees with itself.\n\n* **Same traceback string asserted at two layers.** Test 5 holds\n  both the JSONL channel (live, append-only) and the summary channel\n  (post-run, rendered) in one test. A future refactor that drops the\n  traceback at either layer (e.g., truncating in the summary while\n  keeping it in the JSONL) fails this single test — rather than\n  appearing as a partial pass where one layer guards the other.\n\n* **Cross-link paths are run-relative POSIX.** Per DM-001, every\n  `EvalOutcome.artifacts` value is a POSIX path **relative to the\n  run directory**. The `_per_eval_artifacts_map` helper enforces\n  this when building fixtures: it composes each per-eval file path,\n  takes the `.relative_to(run_dir)`, and converts to POSIX with\n  `.as_posix()`. Test 6 then resolves each value back against the\n  run directory and asserts the resulting path exists. This is the\n  load-bearing convention that makes the artifact tree\n  **relocatable** — tar the run dir, extract anywhere, every\n  cross-link still resolves.\n\n## Reproducibility invariant\n\n```\ncompose_run_id(suite_name, started_at) =\n    f\"{HHMMSSZ}-{sha256(suite_name || ISO-Z-instant)[:8]}\"\n```\n\nThe `sha256(...)[:8]` suffix is the load-bearing reproducibility\nguarantee:\n\n* Same `(suite_name, started_at)` → byte-identical run-id → byte-\n  identical run dir → byte-identical cross-link strings → byte-\n  identical `summary.json`.\n* Different `suite_name` OR different `started_at` → different\n  run-id (collision probability ≈ 2⁻³² per axis, negligible at\n  human-operator scale).\n\nTest 2 pins both halves explicitly. Test 7 reinforces by replaying\nthe same fixture twice and asserting byte-equality on the rendered\n`summary.json` (modulo the run-dir prefix, which is the only\nabsolute path in the tree).\n\n## Why eight tests, not five\n\nThe phase-4 tasklist names five acceptance criteria. The module ships\neight tests:\n\n* Tests 1-5 map 1:1 to AC-1 through AC-4 plus the JSONL existence half\n  of AC-2 (which is two sub-assertions split for diagnostic clarity:\n  \"the file exists\" vs \"the file is JSON-parseable line-by-line\").\n* Test 6 is AC-5 (the cross-link contract).\n* Test 7 is a reinforcement of AC-1 + AC-5: byte-stability across\n  replays — the property that makes \"reproducibility\" mean something\n  observable rather than a layout convention.\n* Test 8 is a negative guard on `parse_run_dir_components` — it raises\n  on paths that do not match the FR-G4 layout. Without this, a future\n  loosening of the parser could silently accept malformed paths and\n  the round-trip in test 1 would still pass. Test 8 is cheap\n  insurance.\n\nThe eighth test could have lived in `test_artifact_layout.py` (T04.13)\nbut the negative guard depends on the same imports and fixture style\nas the other reproducibility tests, so keeping it adjacent reduces\nmaintenance friction.\n\n## Fixture construction\n\n```python\n_STARTED_AT  = \"2026-05-20T15:58:38Z\"  # ISO-Z, fixed for reproducibility\n_FINISHED_AT = \"2026-05-20T15:59:30Z\"  # 52 s after; arbitrary but stable\n_SUITE_NAME  = \"real\"                  # canonical real-suite name\n```\n\nThese constants are module-level so test 2 (deterministic run dir),\ntest 7 (byte-stable replay), and the rest of the module all hash to\nthe same run-id. Changing any of them shifts every assertion in the\nfile.\n\n## Helpers\n\n* `_make_traceback(message)` — raises + catches a `RuntimeError`,\n  returns `traceback.format_exception(...)` joined. Used by test 5\n  and test 7 to build realistic tracebacks.\n* `_write_pass_per_eval(run_dir, eval_id)` — materializes a\n  passing-eval subtree (`logs.jsonl` with two events, an empty\n  `tty.transcript`, an `artifacts/` directory).\n* `_write_errored_per_eval(run_dir, eval_id, traceback_str)` —\n  materializes an ERRORED-eval subtree with the traceback inside the\n  `result.failure` event of `logs.jsonl`.\n* `_per_eval_artifacts_map(run_dir, eval_id)` — builds the\n  run-relative POSIX `Mapping[str, str]` an `EvalOutcome.artifacts`\n  field carries.\n* `_build_summary(run_dir, ...)` — builds a `RunSummary` with\n  `RunCounts` + `RunTotals` consistent with the supplied\n  `EvalOutcome` list; returns the summary + the rendered\n  `summary.json` Path.\n\n## Hand-off to T04.10\n\nWhen T04.10 wires `eval_run` end-to-end:\n\n1. The orchestrator must call `compose_run_dir` / `compose_run_id`\n   for the run-dir layout — tests 1, 2, 7 verify this implicitly.\n2. The per-eval emitter (T03.05) must drop `logs.jsonl` and the\n   PtyDriver (T03.04) must drop `tty.transcript` at the pinned\n   filenames inside `compose_per_eval_dir(...)` — tests 3, 4 verify.\n3. The exception handler must capture `traceback.format_exception(...)`\n   into both the JSONL `result.failure.traceback` event AND the\n   `ExpectFailure.traceback` field rendered into `summary.json` —\n   test 5 verifies.\n4. The `EvalOutcome.artifacts` mapping must contain run-relative\n   POSIX paths to the three well-known names — test 6 verifies.\n\nIf the T04.10 closure satisfies these four steps the entire D-0080\nsuite stays green with no edits here.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}
